# Leads channel, weekend of Oct 24, 2026

Current as of 2026-10-24, 00:00 UTC (opened by the acting CTO; each lead updates its own section)

Sessions cannot message each other, so this file is the one place the leads, the CTO and the owner read. The rule is `.agents/rules/how-we-talk.md` (sessions never message each other; this file, `MERGE-FIRST.md` and `docs/play-first/PROGRESS.md` carry everything):

- **Edit only your own section.** Never touch another section, the header or this list.
- **Newest at the top of your section:** add a new status block above your last one; never rewrite an old one.
- **Every two hours** while you run, and whenever you need something from someone else.
- **Commit straight to main,** with the message `leads: <lead> status <time>` (for example `leads: laws status 14:00 UTC`), the way pool claims push. If the push is rejected, pull with rebase and push again; never force-push.
- **The CTO reads this at every check-in** and relays "Needs from others" to the session that can answer. Every lead PR names its lead in its body (`Lead: laws`).

Each status block has this shape:

```
**Status (HH:MM UTC):** what changed since the last block, with counts from the matrix or the run.
**Needs from others:** who, what, and the item or PR; "none" if nothing.
**Decisions made:** what you decided under the standing rules, and the stopgap or item that carries it.
**PRs:** each open PR in your line, its item and its state (in review, sent back, merged).
```

Proven in Achugao, Northern Mariana Islands (seed 8b3f14260c5df0ef) and Ghent, Minnesota (seed b4e23a2055a3816d, a year): the law reaches the builders, but neither town started a house, so no builder's money moved there.

The voting engine skipped their web links by name while the web kept them as unread rows. The links are now carried and the skip list is deleted. Restoring the vote is wired: laws 70 with #6567. Example chain: Leipsic, Delaware (seed 1042d088fb85644d), law in force January 21; the people barred after their sentence join the eligible voters (reach row `…restore-voting-after-sentence:general:2025-01-21:law-wiring-9af17480:restored…`), the electorate reads it (`group-a-voting.ts:105` from `electorate-inputs.ts:260`), and person_8375d1bf08c01988 heard of it. After the checker's repeal, Delaware's legislature passed HB 22 on March 4 to restore it again. The matrix's repeal grade now tells that apart from a failed repeal. No scene plays it.
- **Open #6577 (housing):** new apartments lowering nearby rents and eviction leading to homelessness were already decided by the housing engine; the links now name it. Links 142.

The pretrial-detention links' notes claim the justice engine decides conviction and employment from detention; it does not, so they stay unreached. The four payroll-tax links into unemployment are about zero by their research. - **Taxes (#5649)** went back at 22:52. Merger 0 found that the family test redraws a town when a law's applied record is missing after play, so a broken producer could be skipped. Fixed on the branch at c94ec2ec24, not yet pushed while its gate runs:
  - a drawn town's eligibility now comes from its opening records (law rows, full and counted workers and owners, a business an organization owns, a public worker, a seated member of the town body);
  - an eligible town with a missing applied record fails the test.
- **Two red CI checks on the merger's head (taxes):**
  - The played-world year check was this PR's. Its test read each paycheck's tax from the end of the posting id, and the PR now ends that id with the law row. Fixed in the test, since the checker reads the law id there.
  - The withholding check is also red on main. It expected state tax from a Missouri worker paid under the state's standard deduction and zero bracket. Fixed here.
- **Justice (#6012)** is green on all nine checks and answers Merger 0's 17:59 points. Its family test has the same redraw problem, so I asked the mergers to hold it until the same fix lands.
- **Prices (#5986)** is green on all nine checks, with all ten laws wired on the new checker. It gets the same test fix, then its body.
- **Rights (#5669) and health and schools (#6026):** helpers are regrading on the new checker.

**Needs from others:** Mergers: hold #6012 and #5986 until I comment with new heads; #5649 after its push.

**Decisions made:**

- A family test decides from the drawn town's opening records whether each law has someone to act on. A missing applied record in an eligible town fails the test; it never redraws.
- The withholding posting id keeps the law row id, because the checker reads it there.

**Decisions made:**

- A law's own payment is looked for among those its records say it paid or charged; a right that names no money (every worker may refuse a union fee) is not expected to show one.
- Of payments shared by as many, the earliest is the law's: a raise's paycheck comes the day before the household spends it.
- Filed: `gov-town-officers-paid-under-minimum-wage` (a small town's officers open at $0.81 to $3.41 an hour, so a minimum wage raise multiplies their pay), four mileage-fee findings and `guards-research-data-reader-red-on-base`.

**Status (Oct 25, 16:05 UTC):** Merger 1 sent labor (#5589) back at 14:21. Five laws' money in the year runs rested on payments I could not tie to them, and Merger 1 asked for each law's own dollar to a named person. The checker now finds that dollar. For each person or business a law's records say it reached, it compares their own payments with the control world's in the weeks after the law reached them. The kind of payment most of them share counts as the law's own: a raise's bigger paycheck, a premium withheld. A money difference that reached no one the law reached now grades partial, not wired. In Uehling, Nebraska, the minimum wage's own payment is a bigger paycheck for 12 of the 18 workers it reached; paid leave's is a premium for 86 of 134. Labor and government are being rerun on it now. Housing (#6011) is green again.

**Needs from others:** Mergers: please hold #5589 until I post that it is ready again, today. The same rule then reaches the other families: 12 laws on the rights, prices, justice, housing and health branches have only untraced money. Each family will be rerun and pushed with the new checker files, one at a time, never while a merger checks it. CTO: still open from before are the screenshot split for `laws-end-to-end-proof`, the prices per-mile gap and dropping `web-police-effect-resets-at-fiscal-year`.

**Decisions made:**

- A law already in force when the world opens is judged where it is not: the checker repeals it in the audit towns, and a repeal lowers no one's pay by design, so no money of its own shows there. For the state wage question and public-sector bargaining, I drew fresh seeds until each was not in force: Cambridge Springs, Pennsylvania (seed `8a0a30a37a505`) and Kingston, Utah (`8a0a30a37a506`). Every place passed over is logged with the reason.
- Engines record a law's money with no consistent sign (a premium withheld is recorded as positive), so the checker reads the direction from the payment itself, not the record.
- The rule's two numbers are set in the checker: 92 days after the law reached someone, and at least one in twenty of the people reached sharing the payment. In Uehling the twin worlds' drift first showed in June, about four months in.

**Decisions made:**

- A benefit decided at the monthly close may lag a household's new job by up to a month. Kept: SNAP's simplified reporting lets a household report a rise by the 10th of the next month (recalled, not checked).
- The year runs show the checker enacting state powers at the city (Medicaid's work requirement, preschool, vouchers, the teacher minimum). The engines read a law at any level, so the grades hold; those rows get regraded when `laws-checker-enacts-where-no-power` lands.
- Filed: `laws-work-requirement-counts-owners-as-idle` (Medicaid's work requirement reads a business owner as working no hours), `schools-world-teacher-counts-by-place` (Washington, D.C.'s schools get 79 teachers, a near-empty Alaska place 99), `web-college-completion-stock-not-accumulated` and `tests-law-reach-group-a-age-check-empty-town` (in #6027).

**Status (Oct 24, 15:15 UTC):** Government (#5582) now holds up over a full played year. In Prescott, Michigan, all ten of its laws grade wired on all nine links over a played year; Uehling, Nebraska's rerun on the final code finishes within the hour (its first year run graded nine of ten, the tenth fixed since). Running the year exposed four real problems, now fixed on the branch. Putting the town's administration budget back a fiscal year after a raise could lay off staff the town opened with, because the cut measured from the staff in place instead of the positions the raise had paid for. A raise carried into a new fiscal year stopped counting its own hires. And the checker read a law's reach only on the year's last day, so a council that repealed or replaced a law in September erased the people it had reached since January. It also read a re-enacted law ten days later, too soon for a law applied at a monthly close. A budget bill also used to lapse at the fiscal year; it now carries into later years as an adopted budget does. The prices and justice year runs are under way, then labor; the housing and health helpers are on their year runs. The end-to-end proof passes on both seeds; main is merged in and its tests are rerunning. On the fresh seed the bill passes over a veto in Burr, Nebraska, a payer who heard the news ties a $4.05 bill to it, and the legislature repeals it five weeks later on its own. Its PR opens after its last test and a speed check.

## Speed

Order: `docs/work-orders/lead-speed-2026-10-24.md`. PR body line: `Lead: speed`.

**Status (Oct 29, 23:40 UTC; speed lead session_01S4WSHpRbBHKwtCjaf1meFB, started by the CTO on the owner's word, ultracode):** Baseline on main 923bfbf3. Two of six places run a year over the two-minute limit: Grand Haven, Michigan (162 s) and Sandy, Utah (135 s). Profiling those two now; the biggest costs get fixed first, one PR each.

**The baseline.** `npm run speed:year`, one process at a time, 4-core cloud container, 15 GB, 64 MiB young generation, one year from Jan 20, 2025:

| Place (key, seed)                                 | Build (s) | Year (s) | Peak memory (MB) |
| ------------------------------------------------- | --------: | -------: | ---------------: |
| Blue Ridge, Indiana (1806022, spd-a)              |       6.4 |     25.5 |            1,625 |
| Washington, District of Columbia (1150000, spd-b) |      32.3 |    102.5 |            2,585 |
| Oviedo, Florida (1253575, spd-c)                  |      25.1 |    109.3 |            2,961 |
| Long Hill, Connecticut (0943580, spd-lh)          |      20.1 |    109.5 |            3,173 |
| Sandy, Utah (4967440, spd-sandy)                  |      26.5 |    134.6 |            2,646 |
| Grand Haven, Michigan (2633340, spd-d)            |      28.8 |    161.8 |            3,674 |

The first four places were drawn from all 56 by seed; Sandy and Long Hill were named by key, as the large towns on the open memory and speed items. Peak memory is the whole process. For the last three it includes the records proof taken after the clock stopped.

**Same records, in dev mode (owner, Oct 29, in this session: "That needs to be built into the advanced mode... trace why things happen"; and "go ahead and develop that and go ahead and merge whatever you need").** Dev mode gets a records proof: one digest per core and engine table at a date, compared between two builds of the same seed and place. A difference names the first record that differs and opens its trace. `speed:year` uses it in place of its old four-number fingerprint (date, people, jobs and money). Every speed PR shows identical proofs on a drawn place.

**How games keep it fast (read for the plan).** Victoria 3 splits each tick into tasks with their own frequency (yearly, monthly, weekly, daily), recomputes only changed ("dirty") modifiers and their dependents, and merges tiny pops back into the population when their count slows employment (Paradox, Victoria 3 Dev Diary #76, "Performance"; #105, migration). We apply the same three where our rules allow: work on the day something changed, never a daily scan; a cache of a value until what it reads changes; and full people only where they matter, with the counted population for everyone else (AGENTS.md, two-level population).

**Needs from others:** none yet. The governing lead's door contacts (a thousand full people per campaign) are its own fix; I measure it when it lands.

**PRs:** the records proof in dev mode (opening now).

**Status (Oct 26, 08:25 UTC):** Two more exact cuts for the later years are open and green: officials' pay and the daily cause updates. A third, checking nearby cause groups first, saved nothing and is recommended for drop. With every open speed change on today's main, Bedford's third year holds within the band at 1.15. Johnstown, Ohio now stops in its third year in the press's weekly staff refresh, which main alone doesn't do, so I'm tracing the person and job it names. Marion, Wisconsin, a second drawn town, runs 1.36.

**The open cuts:**

- **#6145, officials' pay:** each payday finds the town's outside account once. In Johnstown's third year `spendLivingCost` falls from 6.65 to 0.95 s.
- **#6157, cause updates:** in play, the daily rewrite of each fading cause no longer re-checks its source event. In Johnstown's third year `updateDrive` falls from 4.16 to 0.79 s.
- **Both** keep every deep fingerprint identical in Long Hill and over Stanardsville's three years.
- **Not shipped:** `core-speed-drives-offers-filter-first`. Nearly every group on a holder's topic is nearby, so testing nearness first skipped almost no checks (`groupKnown` 26.2 s before, 27.2 s after).

**The band.** One process per town, on main 9bf8ccd9c2 with all nine open speed branches merged:

- **Bedford, Wyoming** (seed 9a4dd331ace7, key 5606045): 38.2, 62.5 and 44.1 s; year three is 1.15 times year one.
- **Marion, Wisconsin** (drawn from all 56 by the same seed, key 5549400): 57.3, 112.7 and 78.0 s; year three is 1.36 times year one. That run had an 8 GB heap, 2 GB less than the others.
- **Johnstown, Ohio** (key 3939340, seed `band-2026-10-26`): years one and two ran 112.1 and 188.2 s, then the run stopped with "A person can only hold their own recorded job". A returning person is handed back a counted job whose job or business has ended. Main alone runs all three years: 132.6, 358.1 and 596.3 s.
- **What the open PRs carry:** main alone's Johnstown third year is 4.5 times its first. The 30-day focus window (#6102) and the slower cause giving (#6082) are what bring it near the band.

**Status (Oct 26, 06:25 UTC):** Long Hill's first year now runs under two minutes in all three runs with every open speed change merged together: 114.4, 112.8 and 116.3 s, against 129.2 s on main on the same machine. The year-three band holds in Bedford (1.12) but not yet in the drawn town, Johnstown, Ohio (1.27). Three exact cuts aimed at Johnstown's third year are built: officials' pay is open as #6145, and the cause offers and the daily cause writes are in their before-and-after runs.

**Long Hill.** Main with #6072, #6074, #6075, #6082, #6102, #6131 and #6133 merged together (0337971f06). Each of the three runs was one process, Long Hill, Connecticut (key 0943580, seed m7b). It becomes true on main as those PRs merge.

**The year-three band.** One process per town, the same combined build:

- **Bedford, Wyoming** (seed 9a4dd331ace7): 31.8, 54.2 and 35.6 s; year three is 1.12 times year one.
- **Johnstown, Ohio** (key 3939340, drawn from all 56 by seed `band-2026-10-26`): 104.4, 181.2 and 132.8 s; year three is 1.27 times year one.
- **Where Johnstown's third year grows,** by profile of years one and three: the drives engine (2.7 to 17.9 s), people's weekly contact ranking (3.1 to 9.7 s), officials' pay (3.2 to 9.1 s) and campaigns (3.1 to 7.0 s). Drives last about six half-lives before they retire, so holders pile up through year three.
- **Year two** is the 2026 election year, 181 s in Johnstown; that is `core-speed-world-races-election-month`, still open.
- **Without #6102** the band is far off: on main alone, Johnstown's profiled years run 128, 329 and 574 s, as the people choosing every day grow from 30 to 317. The 30-day focus window is the change the band needs most.

**The three cuts, each exact:**

**Status (Oct 25, 11:55 UTC):** the year-three band now has sourced numbers. Research (`people-cause-holder-activity-calibrated`, ready on `claude/cause-activity-research`): the AmeriCorps and Census Bureau Civic Engagement and Volunteering Supplement, 2017 to 2023, and the Census Bureau's hours per volunteer. A new report, `scripts/research/cause-activity-year.ts`, checks a played town against them. In Bedford, Wyoming's third year, 63 of 1,643 adults volunteer for a cause (3.8 percent; real people: 23 to 30 percent), at a median 408 hours each (real: 24 to 40). 44 people donate 241 times each (real: 7 to 9 percent of adults give to a political cause), and 115 belong to a group (7.0 percent; real: 24 to 27). Earl Park, Indiana (drawn, seed 991acaa254a988f5) shows the same. Too few people act, and each acts far more often than real people do. That volume is why year three runs slow. **Status (Oct 25, 10:45 UTC):** five speed cuts now wait ready on their branches for the freeze to lift, each exact (deep fingerprints identical for Long Hill, Connecticut's year, seed m7b, and Stanardsville, Virginia's three years), each with its numbers in its item's Notes. New since 08:45:

Two findings for others. Several tests that build a played world run their test worker out of the default 2 GB heap on main: `school-outcomes` and `school-pay` every run, `press-turnover` and two others on large draws. In Bedford, Wyoming's third year, 74 holders take 34,301 cause acts (about 460 each) and 115 members hold 1,906 group memberships (about 17 each), against 10 holders and 209 memberships in year one. That growth is the year-three band's cause.

**Status (04:45 UTC):** first measured cuts. Sequential runs on this machine drift by more than a fix saves (main's own Long Hill year read 220 and 233 s an hour apart), so each fix is now timed by a paired run: both checkouts in one process, each with its own world from the same seed, advanced month by month in alternating order, fingerprints compared at each year's end. An A/A pair (main against main) reads 232.3 and 230.2 s (ratio 0.991), so the method's noise is about 1 percent. Long Hill, Connecticut (seed m7b) one year, base main 185d76d7ce: the cash journal posting less in play (helper, `core-speed-journal-posting-in-play`) 220.0 to 190.3 s (0.865, 30 s); counted births and deaths (`core-speed-counted-renewal-pass`) 245.5 to 235.0 s (0.957, 11 s). Bedford, Wyoming (seed 9a4dd331ace7) three years, alike drive offers (`core-speed-alike-drive-offers`): 0.976, 0.978, 0.960. Every pair's deep fingerprint (each person's money and acts, the counted population, the kept log, ties, journal totals) is identical. Garbage collection: a GC trace shows no full collection of the opening heap during play, only young-generation scavenges (about 16 s a year), so `core-speed-opening-heap-pooled`'s premise does not hold (note in its item). Took over #5433 (counted-household walks without a result object per household) under the bounded item `core-speed-kernel-walks-no-copies`, with the module-table row list kept between writes. The journal helper is on its second item (`core-speed-journal-portfolios-in-play`); the work helper is finishing payroll.

**Ask (05:05 UTC):** #6044 (drives-displaced-prove-oneself-over-band) needs a ruling on its check. Merged since 04:20: #6109 (the Lincoln Memorial at night on the title screen, at native size).

- **Tier fix pushed.** A counted worker's needs were always zero; now they're read the same way as a full worker's. North Canton, Ohio moves from 28.1% to 27.3%. Drives tests pass.
- **Why North Canton stays above 25%.** Every job loss comes from one layoff rule, which lets the newest hires go first in both tiers. Workers who lost jobs held since the opening chose "prove oneself" 21% of the time (18/84). Workers hired through an opening chose it 44% of the time (15/34): they applied because they lean toward the same acts, and they had the least tenure. North Canton reads high because one wholesaler's layoff reached 34 recent hires.
- **Question for the CTO.** Should the 6–25% band hold for every town whatever the mix of who was laid off? Or should the check read the share among jobs held since the opening? Or should last-in-first-out change? It sits until you rule; I'm not refitting toward these towns.

  - Government had no place for someone elected but not yet seated.

## Effects

Order: `docs/work-orders/lead-effects-2026-10-24.md`. PR body line: `Lead: effects`.

Health coverage now follows the economy on main. When a town loses jobs, its employers drop more men's plans than women's, and every household, met or not, follows the same rule. Main has 237 researched links: 136 reach someone, and every other one says why. The check that plays a town twice runs to the end on main's code again. In Paris, Arkansas, 12 of its 17 causes moved other engines' records within four months. The other five wait on long delays or on outcomes no engine reads. Next: a Medicaid expansion stops handing out employer plans.

### What else happened

- **Coverage reads merged.** Its merger reran the outcome web's writers on main, so the fire link's row is on main too. My separate fix for that row would now only add a stale matrix and a duplicate stopgap, so I closed it.
- **Twin check, Paris, Arkansas** (seed `ew20oct26`, four months, all 17 causes, every run passing). Kinds of engine record moved per cause:
  - births 35, broadband 57, corporate rate 55, poor roads 38, unemployment 30, transit 22, violent crime 15, police 10;
  - reported crime, particulates and the rural speed limit 2 each, home prices 1.
  - Nothing moves for the uninsured share (its graduation link lags five years), poverty (its math link lags a year) or graduation (five and ten years). Nothing moves for drinking-water violations or SNAP either: no engine reads their outcomes.
  - The drawn place has passed two of its six runs; its counts follow when the last one ends.
- **The matrix names real sources.** It credited every engine measure to the economy's monthly rows, which pointed police and crime at the wrong table and left the property-tax and corporate-rate readers blank. It now names each one's own reader or table; no grade changes.
- **Medicaid was carried the wrong way.** The new coverage rule reads every link into the uninsured share. The Medicaid expansion and the work requirement are among them, so an expansion would put more workers on employer plans, paying premiums. I am taking those two links off the employer rule. They will name the missing Medicaid plan as their reason, while the job-loss link stays.
- **Schools.** Merger 2 is checking it. Main has since added a reader beside the schools one in the web module, so merging main conflicts. The fix keeps both, and I posted it to the merger rather than pushing.

### Needs from others

- CTO: `web-unwired-fire-link-row` is done on main (the coverage-reads merge carried it); please mark it done.
- Gov lead: `gov-medicaid-coverage-reader` would carry the Medicaid expansion and the work requirement; until it lands, both stay unwired with that reason.
- Mergers: the schools PR, then environment, then the unrun-link grades, transit and roads, the twin-check fix, the matrix sources, and the report last.

### Decisions made

- A link whose outcome's engine carries a different path (Medicaid against employer plans) is skipped by that engine and graded unwired with the gap named; the web still keeps its effect on the outcome.

**Status (06:45 UTC, Oct 27):** Scenes from circumstance work in played years; the PR opens within the hour on branch `claude/director-scenes-from-circumstance`.

- **What it does.** A record written anywhere in the world now calls a scene where the player is, when someone there has a reason from the record: a resident who read that a law took effect stops an officeholder in the street; a household member brings home a story they read; a coworker or neighbor brings up a national outlet's story; someone tied to a person who died tells the player; a colleague catches a member in the hallway after the body sits; an aide calls when a group starts organizing in town. The person is cast by score (named in the record or read it, the player's tie, their sociability), never drawn. The existing scheduler stages it; no second path.
- **Measured** in six drawn worlds played a year each through the player clock (seed director-circ-1): law scenes 21, a printed story 25, a price change 1, a new coworker 8, a death 2, a lost job calling 1, organizing 6, a donor 18. ## Words

## Words and scenes

**Words and director, 02:00 UTC: what you can play now.** Merged since 21:45: someone close to you, often your partner or a relative, can offer to run your campaign, and then they're the one who canvasses with you on election morning. Your journal tells each vote once and says when your bill failed. Open, with the checkers, for the next build:

- when the body you sit on (a council, a selectboard, a county board) votes on your ordinance, the paper prints it (#6831);
- people who read about your bill bring it up with you (#6837);
- when your bill fails, a member who voted for it comes to tell you (#6851);
- taking office is a swearing-in scene, with an address for executives (#6840);
- the debate as a scene you take part in (#6826);
- the paper's side column of short local items (#6824).

### leads: integration status 00:50 UTC (answer to the duplicates session, #6455)

- **Taken in #6462:** the justice engine's own `closeness` (raw level, no fade) is deleted for `closenessBetween`; `catchUpLife` (no caller) is deleted.
- **Campaign row (volunteer-offer vs campaign-stop):** not a twin. `campaign-stop` / `rally-volunteer` is a volunteer already on the team approaching the candidate after a campaign speech (record `speech.heard`); `volunteer-offer` / `offer-help` is someone offering to join (record `volunteer-offered`). The names overlap, the facts do not.
- **Clock, bench hour constants (opening.ts, campaign.ts, results.ts, title.ts, family.ts, places.ts NOON):** these are the minute a screen shows when no packet exists yet (title, opening evening, a results night) rather than a second clock; they stay for now. The player's day start is one parameter (`playerDayStartMinute`) and a scene's hours one table (`settingHours`).
- **C16, wages posted three ways:** yours to take, with thanks: it is the economy's payroll, not the player's job record.

### leads: integration status 00:16 UTC (answer to Screens, campaign board)

All five of the integrator's PRs but one are on main: #6395, #6397, #6403, #6429, #6439; #6412 (ties in scenes) waits on a merger.

**Screens: what the campaign board reads (one source each).**

- **Who is on the team:** `CampaignRow.volunteerIds` in `core2-campaigns-v1` table `campaigns`, keyed by filing id (`campaignFor(api, filingId)`). It is the only membership record; the header count and the Volunteers row already read it (#6403), and the director's offer weight (`volunteer-offered` event, fact `weight`) explains why one joined. **Roles do not exist yet:** `CampaignRow.staffIds` is created empty and never written, and no record gives a volunteer a role (manager, treasurer, canvasser). If the board shows roles, that is a new record on the campaign row (a role per volunteer id), not a second list; ask for it as a pool item with lead: effects (campaigns engine), and the board reads it from `campaignFor` too. A volunteer's history with the player is `sharedHistory(core, playerId, id)` in `src/core2/shared-history.ts` (#6412).
- **Media requests:** `InterviewRow` in `core2-press-v1` table `interviews` (index `byCandidate`): outlet, filing, contest, `askedBy` ("candidate" or "outlet"), date, `status` ("invited", "declined", "published") and the decline `reason`. The outlet's ask is also the `interview-invited` event that stages the scene; the row is the state, the event is the moment.
- **Debate invitations:** `DebateRow` in `core2-meetings-v1` table `debates` (index `byContest`): contest, outlet, date, `status` ("invited", "held"), questions and answers. The ask is the `debate-invited` event (`DEBATE_INVITED_EVENT`); the row is the state.
- **Volunteer offers:** the `volunteer-offered` events in the durable log (the helper is already on `volunteerIds`; a refusal in the scene takes them off). There is no separate offers table, and none should be made.
- **Job offers** (not the campaign, but an inbox item): `JobOfferRow` in the jobs module's `offers` table, now with `offeredBy` (#6429); answered only in its scene.

The office half, probed as a sitting member (Kenton, Oklahoma, seed 4332b41d-c6d3-43a3-ab84-4828a9f6df3c): an ordinance drafts, the board passes it 6 to 1, it takes effect, a cause group forms against it, and a resident stops the player to ask what Ordinance 2025-1 means for them (step 10 plays); no scene shows the change reaching a named person in eleven weeks (step 9 not seen).

## Guards

**leads: guards status 04:25 UTC (done)**: every step of the brief is on main. The one thing left is branch protection, which only the owner's account can set.

- **What now guards the game:** 16 guards run on every pull request and push, judged on the PR merged into main, and the required `validate` check fails when any of them fails. Each failure names the rule in plain words and where to read it.
  - no-dice
  - old-imports
  - no-second-source
  - families: one engine per mechanic; 13 families, CODEOWNERS written from the map
  - two-seeds-differ
  - research-data-has-reader
  - no-module-load-registries
  - one-history
  - english: American English, place and presence, developer text in player strings
  - kit-only-ui
  - canned-content
  - director-numbers-in-data
  - pool
  - reading-line
  - secrets
  - wording
- **Where to read about them:** `docs/design/guards.md` lists every guard, the rule it holds, its file and how to extend it, with the research behind the family boundaries. A test fails if a guard is missing from the page. The merger and local-gate skills point at it.
- **Screen rulings** live in the bench's browser checks, one ruling per check: the title menu, People (no Family tab, no box around a person), the talk box, the card.

  - Residents who say "yes" at the door vote no more often than anyone else: 41 yeses gave 9 votes at 8% turnout in Mount Auburn, IL. Fix built on `governing/door-yes-turnout` (pool `campaign-door-yes-turnout`).
  - Tiny towns' petitions ask more signatures than the town can give (Johnson, MN).
  - The campaign has no groups, volunteers, forum or donor meetings yet. Each is built and in fix or review.

  - #6836 (the yes holds for one household; merger 0 asked for a weighted-town test, done).
  - #6838 (a door fills in at most 2 households, so a season of doors no longer makes a thousand full people).
  - #6839 (M3-01/02/04/05 test commands).
  - #6849 (path-check campaigns to election day and votes for itself).

**Status (15:35 UTC):** main is red on two jobs; four fix PRs are open and none has merged.

- **`two-seeds-differ` guard (`src/core2/realistic-not-real.test.ts`), red since #6600 merged at 14:24 UTC** (runs 40705653361 and 40708711411, the `guards` and `unit` jobs, so every open PR's guards check is red too). Cause: three single cells that two worlds can share by rounding. Row 28, the town's population: Branchville, Virginia, seed 709839a49d0953f4, 119 published and 116 in both worlds (a whole count with a 3% spread has about eight values to land on). Row 27, a campaign price: the same seed's mailer at 16 cents in both worlds; McSherrystown, Pennsylvania, seed 507206323a1e2615, 25 in both; China Town, Northern Mariana Islands, seed 4696288f53883975, a palm card at 17 in both (a cent price with a 30% spread on a 20-cent base). Row 11, local pay: Leusoali'i, American Samoa, seed 1564c1a4803378be, and China Town again: BLS publishes no wages for those territories, so both rows were empty strings. The engine's per-world draws are right; the guard asserted what a whole count or a cent price cannot promise. Fix: **#6625**, the guard compares the town with its state's places, and the market's prices, as rows between worlds (as #6611 did for rents and pay), asserts the data reason for an empty pay row, and compares the states' outlet counts as a row (a fresh draw, Aunu'u, American Samoa, seed 0099ec5284330be1, matched 325 television stations in both worlds); 12 of 12 on every named seed and on ten fresh draws, and CI green on its first head.

Fix: **#6623** restores the script and its test (8 of 8 pass; the script exits 0). #6622 (merged 14:52) also deleted `scripts/core2-year/speed-memory.mjs`, the command of goal spec M7-01: a goal reads missing-command, not a regression; told on #6622.

Read, not fixed: since 9cf11a2d6 (07:01 UTC, chronic conditions drawn per world, factor 1.059 at this seed) each cell's whole count of holders falls differently, and `startingConditionAssignments` spreads holders by personality only inside a cell of place, whole-year age and category; a town's full people make such cells of a few people, so personality has one choice among two or three and cannot show the slope's ratio.
